**Book 220:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas I* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-875-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 1 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (Fase A outcomes and ATP, printed pp. 1–14) plus one guide section per student chapter, with objectives, lesson flow and timing, activities, misconception notes, remedial and enrichment tasks, project rubrics, answer keys keyed to named student-book pages, and reproducible ten-frame, number-bond and game templates (printed pp. 1–200). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. The guide reproduces the student book's chapter covers, placeholder ISBN and all; its own ISBN is as given here. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 221:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas II* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Dara Retno Wulan and Rasfaniwaty, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-898-4. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 2 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance plus one guide section per student chapter, with lesson flow, the two-step word-problem method (known → asked → solution → conclusion) and four keyword cards, misconception handling, parent interaction, assessment rubrics, remedial and enrichment tasks, answer keys and reproducible aids (printed pp. 1–184). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Chapter 1 pages (printed pp. 20–42) and Chapter 2 cover (p. 43) misprint the running footer as "Kelas I"; cite the cover and title page. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 222:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas III* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Hobri, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-936-3. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 3 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table) plus one guide section per student chapter, with teaching guidance, misconception notes and answer keys; its Bab 1 answer-key tables (printed pp. 64–91) key every practice item, not only the chapter tests (printed pp. 1–230). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 223:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas IV* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Hobri, Susanto, Arika Indah Kristiana, Arif Fatahillah, Eko Waluyo, Ridho Alfarisi, Haris Setiya Budi, Moh. Iqbal Helmi, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-244-909-6. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 4 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table with per-topic hour allocations, printed pp. 6–17) plus one guide section per student chapter with objectives, teaching guidance and answer keys (printed pp. 1–252). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Its Bab 1 objectives list prints items 1.1–1.7 only; items 1.8–1.10 (operations and factors/multiples) stand in its ATP table and chapter scheme — an objectives-list omission, not missing teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| The metric system: the basic units are the metre (length), square metre (area), cubic metre (volume), gram (mass) and second (time), with their multiples and submultiples built from powers of ten; converting between units of length, area, volume, mass and time using the powers-of-ten tables (5 km = 5,000 m); choosing the most suitable unit for a measurement (a pupil's mass in kilograms, a gold ring's in grams, a mountain's height, the distance between two cities); scale drawing (مقياس الرسم): the scale is the ratio drawing distance ÷ real distance — the coefficient of proportionality between them, with no unit; using a scale both ways (8 cm representing 80 km gives a scale of 1/1,000,000; a farm plan at 1/500 gives a real area of 47,500 m² from the plan's lengths) | Syria, Grade 7 student's book, printed pp. 108–114 |
| Unit conversions performed with powers of ten (km ↔ m ↔ mm; tonne, decagram, milligram and kilogram; litres and related capacities) | Syria, Grade 8 Algebra student's book, printed p. 44 |
| The distance from a point to a line (مسافة نقطة عن مستقيم) is the length of the perpendicular segment from the point to the line — the foot is the closest point of the line, the distance is zero when the point lies on the line, and of two oblique segments the longer has the farther foot | Syria, Grade 8 Geometry student's book, printed pp. 81–82 |

| Balance puzzles — How Many Oranges: equal oranges and equal papayas on three balances — how many oranges balance the third; Find That Marble: one of 3 marbles is heavier or lighter — find it in only two balancings | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 148 |
| A strip chart of head size — Whose Head is Bigger: paper strips torn at head size pasted in a row; longest strip in cm (biggest head), smallest strip | India, Math-Magic Class 4, printed p. 168 |
| Estimating length — Smallest fish about 1 cm; imagining and comparing lengths of big fish, incl. the whale shark | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 2 |
| Mass — Estimating own mass in kg; mass of 12 children together compared with a whale shark | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 3 |
| Mass and drying — Trek to Gangotri: 1 kg of onions or tomatoes dries to 100 g; ration weights for 6 days for one person and for 10 people | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 197 |
| Mass comparisons — Elephant masses: a 5000 kg elephant in children's weights; a grown elephant eating 136 kg a day — a month, a year | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 199 |
| Mass conversions — Coin sacks: how many 5-rupee coins in 18 kg / 54 kg / 4500 g; 2250 g = 2 kg 250 g; a 2-rupee coin weighs 6 g | India, Math-Magic Class 5, printed p. 200 |
| Unit conversion — Length/mass/capacity unit ladders in word problems (1 km = 10,00,000 mm; kg–g–mg) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 13 |
| Measuring length — Measuring a segment with ruler/divider; betweenness and midpoint checks (AB + BC = AC) | India, Mathematics Class 6, printed p. 89 |
| Number line / ruler jumps — reaching 1 and 2 by unit jumps along a pictured ruler | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 9 |
| Heavy and light by hand — heavy / light (Хүнд хөнгөн): hefting pencil cases and classroom objects, judging with > < = cards; making a tape-roll object heavier/lighter in a photo sequence | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 62 |
| Balance and seesaw comparisons — seesaw pictures: dog against cat, pig, rabbits — which side is heavier; pan balance: 1 apple balances 2 oranges, find what balances a pan of tomatoes | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 63 |
| Capacity in glasses — capacity (Багтаамж): how many glasses of water fill each of two bottles; record "glass — 2" and "glass — 3" for two bottles, three bottles together = 5 glasses | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 71 |
| Capacity comparisons — pouring between containers to compare; a jug = 4 glasses, so 2 jugs = ? glasses; a Grade-1 child needs 5 glasses of water a day — how many for two children? | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 72 |
| Measuring length (Уртыг хэмжих): which pencil is longer; a pencil = 7 buttons, a brush = 9 buttons; a pen = 5 paperclips, a card measured in clips; photos: scissors and a shoe measured with connector bricks | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 99 |
| Centimetre — the centimetre (1 сантиметр, см): a segment (Хэрчим) is measured in centimetres; a pencil measures 4 cm on a ruler from 0; write the lengths of three pencils in cm; draw segments of 3, 6, 1, 9, 5, 7 cm | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 100 |
| Lengths on squared paper — lengths in grid cells converted to cm (8 cells = 4 cm); a 14 cm segment and shorter segments measured; which path is longer — straight vs stepped lines on a grid; distances between a ladybird, mushroom, flower and bee read off a 0-20 cm ruler | Mongolia, Математик I (Grade 1), printed p. 101 |
| Revision: centimetre and seasons — recall: length is measured in centimetres, written 1 cm; which season is each picture? (sheep shearing, snowman, harvest, flowers); in which season is New Year, when is greenery planted/harvested, in which season does rain fall | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 8 |
| Perimeter by multiplication (equal sides) — which pictured part matches 5 × 4 (bags of sweets); how many wheels have the boy's cars in all?; children built shapes from wire with all sides equal: a pentagon with 3 cm sides, a hexagon with a 6 cm side, a pentagon with a 4 cm side — how many centimetres of wire did each shape use?; compute mentally (products of 2 and 5, of 5 and 3, of 4 and 5, of 5 and 5); a five-tree bird problem in five parts (3 birds in each tree — in all?; 4 more birds settle in each tree — how many came?; total now?; 2 birds fly off from each tree — how many flew off?; more or fewer than at first?) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 43 |
| Measuring length: three friends measured a book's length with a ruler — who measured correctly (Bat / Oyuu / Bold ruler positions)?; the rule box: put the ruler's 0 mark at the segment's start and lay the ruler along it, read the mark at the segment's end; how many centimetres are the two pictured segments (rulers shown under them)?; measure segments with a ruler and write the measures in the notebook; draw segments of 6 cm, 9 cm, 11 cm (rule box: lay the ruler down, mark the start and end points, join them with a straight line) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 51 |
| Millimetre — the girl measured her glue stick: more than 8 cm, less than 9 cm — is there a suitable smaller unit?; a centimetre divided into ten equal parts: each part's length is 1 millimetre, written 1 mm; the glue is 8 cm 7 mm long; 1 cm = 10 mm; which of the ribbons is 4 cm 6 mm long?; measure your own pencil and pen; a segment of 5 cm 4 mm: 5 cm 4 mm = 54 mm (50 mm and 4 mm); express segment lengths in cm and mm, then in mm only | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 52 |
| Decimetre — draw segments of 8 mm, 15 mm, 22 mm; distances between desk objects: scissors to glue 11 cm 5 mm, glue to eraser 6 cm, total 11 cm 5 mm + 6 cm = 17 cm 5 mm — numbers with the same unit are added; the difference 11 cm 5 mm − 6 cm = 5 cm 5 mm; conversions (8 cm 9 mm = □ mm; 1 cm 5 mm = □ mm; 3 cm = □ mm; 23 mm = □ cm □ mm; 40 mm = □ cm; 34 mm = □ cm □ mm); arithmetic (6 cm 2 mm + 2 cm; 12 cm + 3 cm 5 mm; 7 cm 8 mm − 5 cm…); the girl's blue ribbon is 10 cm: ten 1 cm ribbons joined equal it — a 10 cm measure is called 1 decimetre, written 1 dm; 10 cm = 1 dm | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 53 |
| Decimetre and centimetre together — the boy marked from his elbow to his fingertip on the desk and measured: 2 dm 3 cm = 23 cm (20 cm and 3 cm); his hand-span (төө) is 14 cm long, so 14 cm = □ dm □ cm; measure three segments and write their measures, the smallest in another unit; measure your desk top's width in dm and cm; draw segments of 2 dm, 1 dm 2 cm, 2 dm 4 cm; a mouse's route: from its hole to the plate 2 dm 5 cm and 4 dm more — 2 dm 5 cm + 4 dm = 6 dm 5 cm; from the plate to the cheese 6 dm 5 cm + 3 dm 3 cm = 9 dm 8 cm — which of the hole-to-plate and hole-to-cheese distances is shorter? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 54 |
| Metre — the boy cut a ribbon using his arm-span: the ribbon measures 30 cm + 30 cm + 30 cm + 10 cm = 100 cm; a length equal to 100 centimetres is called 1 metre, written 1 m; the metre is the basic unit for measuring length; my measured ribbon is 1 m long; 100 cm = 1 m; measuring tools: the tape measure (тууз метр), the folding metre, the ruler, the set square (гурвалжин шугам); measure and record: the maths notebook's width with a ruler, the classroom's width with a tape measure — for each width say in which unit it was expressed | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 55 |
| Unit relations and conversions — measure the classroom desk top and board with a 10 dm string; a length of 10 dm is 1 metre (1 m); the summary box — the smallest unit for measuring length is the millimetre (mm): 1 cm = 10 mm, 1 dm = 10 cm, 1 m = 10 dm; conversions (9 cm = □ mm; 1 m = □ dm; 85 cm = □ dm □ cm; 78 cm = □ dm □ cm; 10 dm = □ m; 38 mm = □ cm □ mm); measure arithmetic (7 m 3 cm + 3 m; 14 m + 2 m 8 cm; 9 m + 2 m 7 cm; 4 m 9 cm − 5 cm; 22 m 6 cm − 6 cm; 5 m 4 cm − 2 m 2 cm); word problems: Tuya was to buy 5 dm of ribbon but the seller gave her a ribbon 1 dm longer — how many centimetres of ribbon did Tuya get?; a rectangular vegetable field is 20 m wide and its length is 20 dm more than its width — the field's length in metres?; how many centimetres in 1 metre, how many millimetres in 1 decimetre — worked example: express 3 cm in millimetres: 1 cm = 10 mm so 3 cm = 3 × 10 mm = 30 mm; follow the pictures to measure arm-span and height | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 56 |
| Traditional body measures — fill in (2 dm = □ cm; 5 cm = □ mm; 6 m = □ dm; 8 cm = □ mm); observe how the operations were done in bar models (7 m + 13 m = 20 m = 200 dm; 11 dm − 7 dm = 4 dm = 40 cm); practical work: cut a 10 cm string, cut a 1 dm string, compare the cut strings' lengths; traditional national measures shown in pictures: сөөм, төө (span), тохой (cubit), дэлэм, алд (arm-span), алхам (step); measure your own сөөм, төө, тохой, дэлэм, алд into a table; an adult's approximate measures are tabulated (төө 18-22 cm, тохой 32-40 cm, дэлэм 70-80 cm, алхам 80-100 cm) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 57 |
| Length arithmetic in mixed units — Solongo has a 10 cm ribbon and Bat has a 40 cm ribbon — their ribbons' total length is how many decimetres?; find the distance from school to home on the route picture (school to shop 150 m, shop to hospital 200 m, hospital to home 400 m); observe how the operation was done with bar strips and explain (2 m 50 cm + 1 m 10 cm = 3 m 60 cm; 2 m 30 cm − 1 m 10 cm = 1 m 20 cm); write the following length measures in your notebook in decreasing order (15 dm; 125 cm; 3 m; 78 mm; 65 m; 600 cm; 35 dm; 100 mm); compare (25 dm … 250 mm; 300 cm … 3 m; 45 cm … 5 dm; 10 m … 110 dm) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 105 |
| Measuring weight (Хүндгийг хэмжих): an experiment — each group prepares 1 potato, 1 book, 1 ruler, 1 nut on its desk; each group member lifts the prepared things one by one and estimates and compares heavy and light (how do you know which is heavy? It can be known by lifting. It can also be measured); what instruments are there for measuring weight? Is there an instrument not shown in the picture among the ones named? (a platform scale, an electronic scale, a balance, a spring scale, a kitchen dial scale, a bathroom scale pictured); observe the pan-scale readings and discuss (bags of flour on dial scales); the basic unit for measuring weight is the kilogram, and kilogram is written shortened as "кг" | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 118 |
| Kilogram problems — observe the picture and estimate 1 kg measures (1 kg of bananas, a 1 kg bag of sugar, 1 kg of cotton, a 1 kg stone, 1 kg of apples — the same weight with very different bulks); if 1 kg of flour balances 6 apples, how many apples do 5 kg of flour balance?; how many kilograms do the dials show (five scale faces)?; the picture shows the weights of Bat's and Naran's family members (Bat's family: father 85 kg, mother 65 kg, Bat 25 kg; Naran's family: father 80 kg, mother 58 kg, elder sibling 22 kg, Naran 18 kg): a. find the sum of the weights of Bat's father and mother and of Naran's father and mother, and compare them; b. find Bat's and Naran's families' total weights and compare; c. using the picture, compose a problem; the girl weighs 25 kg: she sat on the scales, stood, and lifted one leg — does her weight change? discuss; a group task "Let's measure weight": materials — electronic scales and paper for recording the weights; the class is divided into 5 groups; each group chooses 1 book, 1 pen and 1 bag and weighs them; discuss the results | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 119 |
| Measuring liquid (Шингэнийг хэмжих): two children took vessels to water the flowers; to know their vessels' capacities they filled them by pouring with a cup and emptied them: the boy's vessel filled after pouring 4 times with his cup, the girl's after 5 times with hers — whose vessel has the bigger capacity? The children's water cups have different capacities, so they cannot compare the vessels' capacities with them; to know a liquid's measure, a vessel of 1 litre capacity is used: liquid is measured with the unit called litre, written "л"; 1 litre of water was poured 5 times into the boy's vessel, so his vessel's capacity is 5 litres; how many litres is the girl's vessel (three 1-litre jugs pictured)? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 120 |
| Litre problems — how many litres is each pictured vessel's capacity (a kettle, a pot, a bucket, a bowl — counted from 1-litre tiles beside each)?; the boy measured water into 5 vessels — determine each vessel's amount of water from the graduated pictures (scales marked 1-6 litres); using a 1-litre vessel, find the capacities of various vessels at home; estimate the capacities of various vessels and say them, then check with a 1-litre vessel whether the estimate matched (a table: a pot estimated 8 litres, the measuring vessel, the checked measure 6 litres); mother and daughter milked the cow together: mother milked 15 litres of milk and the daughter milked 6 litres less than mother — how many litres of milk did they milk in all?; compare (100 l − 65 l … 81 l − 49 l; 18 l + 33 l … 91 l − 42 l); the boy bought 10 litres of airag on the first day and 2 times more airag than that the next day — how many litres of airag did he buy in all?; Saran will fill a vessel of 11 litres capacity with milk: if Saran has a bucket of 3 litres capacity, how many times must she pour to fill it? | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 121 |
| Consolidation: perimeter journeys, seasons — a hare (Бүжин) and a squirrel started from home at the same time: passing the given shapes (a route drawn around houses, a circle, a rhombus, rectangles with the distance between shapes 3 m), one went around the shapes and the other went in the direction between them: how many metres of road did the hare travel? how many metres did the squirrel travel? who of the squirrel and the hare travelled more road, and why? If the hare went from home, went the whole way around each shape and came back to its first place, how many metres of road would it travel?; Solongo's and Khasar's body weights are equal to Khaliun's and Tulga's weights: Khasar is 36 kg and Tulga is 42 kg — which of Solongo and Khaliun is heavier?; a gardener planted 92 trees in three days: on the first day he planted 34 trees and on the second day 27 trees — how many trees did he plant on the third day?; soft sweets were packed in 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30, 35, …, 85, 90, 95 per packet: from 100 sweets, in how many ways can packets be taken (examples: 5 + 95 = 100; 5 + 10 + 85 = 100)?; which season do the pictured activities belong to — discuss (a lesson, swimming, a forest walk, a market scene); strengthen what you learned in grade 2 and study successfully in grade 3 (closing line) | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 151 |
| Measuring length (Уртыг хэмжих): pictures of a picture frame, a foot being measured for a shoe, a vest, a room plan and window curtains: what is measuring length needed for? Observe the pictures and discuss; estimate the table's length (төөлөөрэй): how many pencils came out? When the table's length was measured with a pencil, did the same number come out as when measured with spans (төө)? Which is the table's length? Why? A girl's speech: "Depending on the unit chosen, the measure of a length differs. So when writing a measurement, the measurement's unit is written after the number. For example: 4 pencils, 5 spans"; what do you know about the tools used for measuring length (pictures: a set square, dividers, a folding wooden metre rule, a tape measure, a tailor's measuring tape, a trundle measuring wheel, a height measure, a 1 m mark)?; which of the segments is 1 cm long, which is 1 mm, which is 1 dm? (a ruler picture from 0 to 11 cm with three segments above it); when measuring a segment's length, which placing of the ruler is right (two pictures: one with the segment starting at the ruler's 0, one starting at 1)?; write the lengths in centimetres and millimetres (a nail pictured on a ruler: 3 cm … mm; a pencil pictured on a ruler: … cm … mm) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 68 |
| Drawing segments; the length units and their relations — measure the segments' lengths and write the measurements in the units centimetre and millimetre (five coloured segments); find what length all the segments together make; draw segments of the lengths 2 cm, 1 cm 3 mm, 1 cm 7 mm, 3 cm 5 mm and 1 dm; how a segment is drawn using a divided ruler (the instruction box: place the segment to be drawn on top of the ruler; place the segment's beginning at the ruler's 0 division; mark the segment's end point; connect the points using the ruler and draw the segment; write the measurement — the example shows a pencil of 3 cm 6 mm); a boy's speech: "On a measuring tool, count the divisions between the numbers in the measurement" (a ruler 0-3 cm is pictured); make from paper one strip each of 1 cm, 1 dm and 1 m length: compare the lengths: express the bigger unit with the smaller unit; using the paper strip you made or a measuring tool, find roughly how many metres: imagine laying 10 strips of 1 m length joined end to end — what length would it be? How long is 10 m? Does the classroom's length reach 10 m? How long are 100 m and 1000 m? Can you imagine them?; the units boxes: the length units are kilometre (км), metre (м), decimetre (дм), сантиметр — centimetre (см), millimetre (мм); the relations between the length units: 1 km = 1000 m; 1 m = 10 dm = 100 cm; 1 dm = 10 cm = 100 mm; 1 cm = 10 mm; a girl's speech: "The main unit for measuring length is the metre"; for the things pictured (a road, a roll of paper, a paperclip, a screw), which unit is it suitable to measure the length with? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 69 |
| Converting length units; arithmetic with measures — write the numbers and measures the arrows point to in the picture in your notebook (a. write the number: arrows on number-line sections 270-300 pointing to 276, 90-120 pointing to 94, 1530-1560, and 3800-3830; b. express the measures in cm and mm: arrows on rulers pointing to 8 cm 7 mm and 21 cm 3 mm); worked example 1: explain how a measurement is expressed from one unit into another (a. 4 cm = (4 × 10) mm = 40 mm; b. 15 m = (15 × 100) cm = 1500 cm; c. 3 km = (3 × 1000) m = 3000 m; d. 70 dm = (70 ÷ 10) m = 7 m; e. 600 cm = (600 ÷ 100) m = 6 m; f. 10000 m = (10000 ÷ 1000) km = 10 km); find the matching number and write it (8 m = □ dm; 4 m = □ cm; 5 dm = □ mm; 2000 m = □ km; 1400 cm = □ m; 240 dm = □ m; 900 cm = □ m; 30 m = □ cm); worked example 2: explain how the measurement's unit was converted (a. 5 km 230 m = 5 km + 230 m = (5 × 1000) m + 230 m = 5000 m + 230 m = 5230 m; b. 2470 m = 2000 m + 470 m = (2000 ÷ 1000) km + 470 m = 2 km 470 m); write in the given unit (8 dm 9 cm = □ cm; 1 m 6 cm = □ cm; 74 cm = □ dm □ cm; 1 km 50 m = □ m; 38 mm = □ cm □ mm; 1738 m = □ km □ m); compare (7 dm 1 cm □ 7 dm 9 mm; 125 cm □ 1 m 2 dm; 8 m 5 dm □ 8 m 49 cm; 3 km 7 m □ 3 km 70 dm; 2 m □ 20 dm; 4 dm □ 41 cm); round the measurements and write them: a. in centimetres: 5 cm 8 mm; 7 cm 4 mm; 3 cm 5 mm; 18 cm 7 mm; 65 mm; b. in metres: 3 m 5 dm; 2 m 80 cm; 1 m 39 cm; 1 m 9 cm; 71 dm; 460 cm; c. in kilometres: 7 km 350 m; 4 km 760 m; 1862 m; 2460 m; worked example 3: explain the calculations (415 + 227 = 642 alongside 4 m 15 cm + 2 m 27 cm = 6 m 42 cm; 415 − 227 = 188 alongside 4 m 15 cm − 2 m 27 cm = 1 m 88 cm; 974 × 6 = 5844 alongside 974 m × 6 = 5844 m; and the long division 296 m ÷ 8 = 37 m with its grid working) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 70 |
| Measure arithmetic; perimeter named — calculate and explain (593 cm + 204 cm; 4907 m − 3495 m; 204 cm + 99 cm; 320 km − 168 km; 13 m 90 cm + 59 m 34 cm; 5 km 785 m + 2 km 127 m; 50 m 37 cm − 29 m 45 cm; 8 dm 5 cm − 39 cm; 2 km 300 m × 4; 17 cm 9 mm × 9; 4 m 50 cm ÷ 9; 1 km 827 m ÷ 7); write the measures in decreasing order (1000 m, 1 km 3 m, 1200 m; 2200 mm, 225 cm, 23 dm, 2 m; 1 m 76 cm, 18 dm, 177 cm; 40 dm, 4 m 3 cm, 401 cm); estimate a thing's length and write it, then check the estimate by measuring with a tool: a. the lengths of classroom tools and a pencil case — estimate and measure to check; b. the length, width and height of household things — estimate, then measure and check; measure the length of a broken (bent) line and write the measurement in centimetres and millimetres (four open polylines: a V-shape, a zigzag, an almost-rectangle, a triangle-like line and a hexagon outline); measure the sides' lengths and find the shape's boundary length (three shapes drawn on grid paper: a notched rectangle; a stepped shape; a boat-like shape): a boy's speech names it: "A shape's boundary length is called its perimeter (периметр)"; a test (Сорих): write the segments' lengths in centimetres and millimetres (two segments pictured on rulers); draw segments of lengths 3 cm, 1 cm 5 mm, 2 cm 7 mm, 1 cm 8 mm and 1 dm 1 cm; complete the writing (6 dm 7 cm = □ cm; 95 cm = □ dm □ cm; 48 mm = □ cm □ mm); approximately express the measure with the bigger unit (9 cm 5 mm; 6 m 34 cm; 7 dm 9 cm; 5 km 42 dm as printed); arrange in order from smallest to biggest (1 m 57 cm; 6 dm 8 cm; 65 cm; 8 cm 9 mm); a pupil wrapped the first box with 1 m 59 cm of ribbon and the second box with 143 cm of ribbon: find the difference between the two ribbons' lengths; find the total length of the two ribbons | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 71 |
| Consolidation (Бататгах): write each plant's height measurement (five plants pictured against centimetre rulers, the tallest about 110 cm); express the tallest plant's measurement in metres and centimetres: by how much is the tallest plant taller than the others?; Tuya is 124 cm tall, Bat is 1 m 27 cm and Bilgüün is 1 m 19 cm: a. arrange the heights in order from biggest to smallest: show the method of finding them on a tape measure; b. find the pupils' height differences approximately; one pupil said "A person's arm-span (алд) and height measurements are approximately equal": what do you think? Check it and explain; the distances between Ulaanbaatar city and the provinces are shown in a table (a triangular distance table in kilometres: Ulaanbaatar–Arkhangai 477; Ulaanbaatar–Uvs 1417; Arkhangai–Uvs 940; Ulaanbaatar–Sükhbaatar 565; Arkhangai–Sükhbaatar 1042; Uvs–Sükhbaatar 1982; and the Selenge row 321, 617, 1361, 886): a. Arkhangai is at a distance of 1042 km from Sükhbaatar province: explain how this was found from the table; b. at what distance from Ulaanbaatar is Sükhbaatar province?; c. tourists came from Uvs province to Ulaanbaatar and travelled from there to Arkhangai — find the total length of road they travelled; d. using the table, compose problems and solve them; measure the shapes' side lengths: find the shapes' perimeters (a pentagon, a rhombus, a triangle and a hexagon drawn as outlines) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 72 |
| Weighing: scales and the gram — measuring weight (Хүндийг хэмжих): pictures of a juice bottle, an apple, a watermelon, a car and a dump truck: what is in the pictures? What units and tools for measuring their weight (хүнд) do you know? Learn the ones you don't know together; discuss: a boy holds up a ruler and a pencil: which is heavier? A girl asks: "Can you know by holding them in your two hands?"; a boy's speech: "There are very many kinds of measuring tool made for various purposes. For example, the pan balance (тавган жинлүүр). A hanger (өлгүүр) can be made using string and a small paper cup"; can the weight be known with a pan balance? Why?; two scales are pictured: a household scale (ахуйн жинлүүр) with capacity (даац) 3 kg to 5 kg, and a hand scale (гар жинлүүр) with capacity 1 kg to 10 kg; a girl's speech: "A weight measurement is indicated by a pointer (заадагч) and divisions (хуваарь)"; write the weight measurements of the granulated sugar and the flour (two platform scales pictured with sacks on them); what should be paid attention to when using a measuring tool? Before measuring, what should the pointer be at? How are the divisions read? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 95 |
| Kilograms and grams; mass-unit relations — things to pay attention to when using a balance (a box): 1. place the balance on a flat surface; 2. look at whether the pointer is indicating the 0 division; 3. look at the divisions the pointer indicates; 4. a thing heavier than the capacity cannot be weighed; a girl adds: "For a hand scale, pay attention to steps of more than 2 (kg)"; measure the weight of a book, a bag (цүнх) and a watermelon and write the measurements (three dial scales are pictured with the items on them); a girl's speech: "There is a unit called the 'gram' (грамм) for measuring weight. It is written in short as 'г'"; a boy explains a scale dial: the part from 0 to 250 is divided into 5 equal parts by the divisions: 250 ÷ 5 = 50, so the part between the divisions is 50 g: the pointer is indicating the 3rd division, that is, 150 g: the book's weight measurement is 150 g; an activity: compare the measurements 1 kilogram and 1 gram: prepare sand or rice, 1 g and 1 kg weights (туухай), a balance and a bag: a. put about 1 kg of sand in one bag and about 1 g of sand in another bag; b. check your bags' weight measurements using the measuring tool; c. what action did you do so that the measurements became exactly 1 kg and 1 g? Discuss; d. lift the 1 kg and the 1 g of sand and compare the weight measurements; observe and discuss (two boxes): the weight measurement units are tonne (т), centner (ц), kilogram (кг), gram (г) and milligram (мг); the relations between the weight units: 1 t = 10 centners = 1000 kg; 1 centner = 100 kg; 1 kg = 1000 g; 1 g = 1000 mg; a boy's speech: "The kilogram is the main unit for measuring weight"; if the weight measurement of one sack of flour is 20 kg, how many sacks of flour have a weight measurement of 1 centner? Of 1 tonne (5 sacks; 50 sacks)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 96 |
| Converting mass units; arithmetic with masses — get to know the tools for measuring things' weight (pictures with capacities: a pan balance — capacity up to 100 g; a small scale — capacity 500 g; a trade scale — capacity 30 kg; a trade scale — capacity 10 kg; a platform scale — capacity 180 kg; a car scale (пүү) — capacity up to 200 tonnes; a platform scale — capacity 250 kg); worked example 1: explain how the measurement unit was converted (a. 6 t = (6 × 10) centners = 60 centners; b. 2 centners = (2 × 100) kg = 200 kg; c. 3 kg = (3 × 1000) g = 3000 g; d. 80 centners = (80 ÷ 10) t = 8 t; e. 400 kg = (400 ÷ 100) centners = 4 centners; f. 5000 kg = (5000 ÷ 1000) t = 5 t); find the matching number and write it (7 centners = □ kg; 12 t = □ centners; 9 g = □ mg; 8000 mg = □ g; 2300 kg = □ centners; 580 centners = □ t; 7000 g = □ kg; 10 kg = □ g); worked example 2: explain how the measurement unit was converted (a. 2 kg 300 g = 2 kg + 300 g = (2 × 1000) g + 300 g = 2300 g; b. 2400 g = 2000 g + 400 g = (2000 ÷ 1000) kg + 400 g = 2 kg 400 g); write in the given unit (3 kg 20 g = □ g; 2500 g = □ kg □ g; 2 g 500 mg = □ mg; 240 kg = □ centners □ kg; 4 t 5 centners = □ centners; 6700 kg = □ t □ kg); compare (8 kg 500 g □ 8 kg 501 g; 3200 g □ 3 kg 20 g; 2 t □ 20 centners; 7 g 100 mg □ 7010 mg; 4 centners 80 kg □ 479 kg; 1 t 30 kg □ 1 t 3 centners); worked example 3: explain the calculations (2500 + 1800 = 4300 alongside 2 kg 500 g + 1 kg 800 g = 4 kg 300 g; 2500 − 1800 = 700 alongside 2 kg 500 g − 1 kg 800 g = 700 g; 974 × 6 = 5844 alongside 974 g × 6 = 5844 g; and the long division 296 kg ÷ 8 = 37 kg with its grid working); calculate and explain (34 kg + 91 kg; 3 kg 200 g − 1 kg 600 g; 5700 g + 2900 g; 3 centners 58 kg × 9; 3 kg 200 g + 1 kg 600 g; 4 kg 581 g ÷ 9) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 97 |
| Mass consolidation; weighing with weights — consolidation (Бататгах): write the measurements (five scale dials are pictured, in kilograms and in grams); with which unit and which tool is it suitable to measure the weight of: a person's body, a passenger car, a bag, a ball, an eraser, a pen, and one grain of white rice?; write the measures in increasing order: find the difference between the biggest and the smallest measures; round the measures to tonnes (a. 2000 kg, 2 t 3 centners, 47 centners; and the mixed sets carried over from earlier chapters: times and lengths); write the measures in decreasing order and round them to kilograms (3 kg 350 g, 3020 g, 3750 g; 4 kg 832 g, 4160 g, 3980 g, 5 kg 90 g); write the weight measurements of 5 pupils of the class: a. show the pupils' weight measurements with a bar diagram; b. find the difference between the biggest and the smallest weight measurements; measure a bag and the things regularly inside it: which things that are not needed for school are there in the bag? If those things are taken out of the bag, by how much will the bag's weight measurement decrease?; a boy measured the weights of 3 fruits: an apple is 266 g and an orange is 72 g less than that; a banana's weight is equal to half of the apple's: show the fruits' weight measurements with a bar diagram; calculate and explain: a. Tsetseg bought 2 kg 400 g of potatoes and 500 g of carrots: find the difference between the weight measurements of the carrots and the potatoes she bought; find the total weight measurement; b. a vegetable grower harvested 237 kg of tomatoes: if the tomatoes are packed into 6 kg boxes, how many boxes will be filled? How many boxes are needed?; an activity: what things can be measured using a balance without a scale (хуваарьгүй жинлүүр) and weights of 100 g, 300 g and 900 g, by balancing them (the example pictures show honey jars balanced against a 400 g weight, and bananas against a 200 g weight)?; a self-check: compare the measures and find their sums' and differences' values (3 kg 750 g and 5600 g; 3 centners 70 kg and 290 kg; 2 t 894 kg and 1247 kg) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 98 |
| The litre and millilitre; converting volumes — measuring volume (Эзлэхүүнийг хэмжих): two children bought buckets of the same capacity at a shop: to find their buckets' capacity, they filled the bucket with water using their drinking vessel: the boy: "When I poured water 9 times with my drinking vessel, the bucket filled"; the girl: "When I poured 6 times with my drinking vessel, the bucket filled"; which bucket's capacity do 9 and 6 express?; a boy's note: "When measuring, the result changes depending on the capacity of the vessel used. Therefore, whatever unit measure it is, the number must be written after it"; discuss what units and tools there are for measuring liquid (pictures of syringes, a measuring cup and milk cartons); a girl's speech: "A liquid of small measure is measured in 'millilitres'. For example, a cup's capacity, a syringe's measure and so on"; what vessels of 1 litre capacity do you know (pictures of a bottle, a thermos, a bottle, a jar and pots)?; a box: the units for measuring liquid are the litre (л) and the millilitre (мл): 1 l = 1000 ml; a boy's speech: "The litre is the main unit for measuring liquid"; measure each vessel's capacity with a 1-litre vessel and depict it (pictures of a bucket, a metal pail, a big water container, a green bottle and a teapot, with tallies of 1 l measures beside them): write each vessel's capacity measurement from smallest to biggest; write the liquid's volume measurement (four graduated vessels pictured with liquid levels against 1 l, 2 l, 3 l lines); express the liquid's measure in litres and millilitres and write it (the example: in picture б there is 2 litres 700 ml of liquid): if 500 ml of liquid is added to each vessel, what will the volume measurement become?; worked example 1: explain how the measurement unit was converted (a. 2 l = (2 × 1000) ml = 2000 ml; b. 2 l 350 ml = 2 l + 350 ml = 2000 ml + 350 ml = 2350 ml; c. 7000 ml = (7000 ÷ 1000) l = 7 l; d. 7480 ml = 7000 ml + 480 ml = 7 l + 480 ml = 7 l 480 ml); find the matching number and write it (3 l = □ ml; 8 l = □ ml; 5 l = □ ml; 2000 ml = □ l; 6000 ml = □ l; 10000 ml = □ l; 1 l 670 ml = □ ml; 9 l 51 ml = □ ml; 3 l 859 ml = □ ml) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed pp. 108–109 |
| Arithmetic with litres and millilitres — find the matching number and write it (4500 ml = □ l □ ml; 5470 ml = □ l □ ml; 9023 ml = □ l □ ml; 6142 ml = □ l □ ml; 1003 ml = □ l □ ml; 7529 ml = □ l □ ml); arrange in order from biggest to smallest (1700 ml, 6040 ml, 4630 ml; 3 l 25 ml, 4 l 20 ml, 3 l 40 ml; 3 l 250 ml, 3750 ml, 3 l 200 ml; 1329 ml, 8100 ml, 6788 ml, 678 ml; 3 l 635 ml, 2 l 950 ml, 4 l 900 ml; 2 l 635 ml, 4 l 560 ml, 5020 ml); worked example 2: explain the calculations (5830 + 2290 = 8120 alongside 5 l 830 ml + 2 l 290 ml = 8 l 120 ml; 6100 − 3250 = 2850 alongside 6 l 100 ml − 3 l 250 ml = 2 l 850 ml; 1732 × 4 = 6928 alongside 1 l 732 ml × 4 = 6 l 928 ml; and the long division 291 l ÷ 3 = 97 l shown in its grid); calculate and explain (1300 ml + 500 ml; 1800 ml − 600 ml; 3718 ml + 4 l 562 ml; 7 l 650 ml + 1 l 49 ml; 6 l 200 ml − 2 l 950 ml; 5400 ml + 1 l 750 ml; 3 l 150 ml − 1569 ml; 379 ml × 5; 1 l 223 ml × 3; 1 l 685 ml × 5; 1 l 350 ml × 4; 726 ml ÷ 6; 1 l 428 ml ÷ 4); an activity: measure in millilitres: a. prepare paper cups, empty and of various measures, and unmarked syringes; b. the pupils draw water from a paper cup with a syringe 5 times and put it into an empty cup; c. calculate the total measure of the water drawn with the syringe (the example: Tuya drew water with a syringe in measures of 3 ml, 2 ml, 3 ml, 3 ml and 1 ml: Tuya drew 12 ml of water in total); a vessel has 18 l of water: if the following measures are poured from it into another vessel, how many litres of water will remain in the vessel (2 l; 3 l; 6 l; 9 l; 10 l; 2 l and 3 l)?; one vessel has 2 l 150 ml of tea and the other vessel has 1 l 670 ml more tea than the first: find the total measure of the tea in both vessels; a self-check: 1) find the measures (6 l 487 ml + 3296 ml; 4 l 230 ml − 1 l 680 ml; 1 l 350 ml × 4; 1 l 320 ml ÷ 6); 2) a vessel has 3 l 450 ml of water: 1785 ml of it is poured into another vessel: how much water is left in the vessel? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 110 |
| Volume consolidation: estimating capacity — consolidation (Бататгах): express the liquid measures in litres and millilitres and write them (five graduated vessels are pictured with liquid levels and mixed labels: 3 l, 2 l 500 ml, 6 l, 6 l, 5 l vessels); write the measures in order from smallest to biggest and find the difference between the biggest and smallest measures (a. 2 l 380 ml, 1 l 50 ml, 1504 ml; b. lengths: 120 cm, 2 m 6 cm, 10 dm 3 cm; c. weights: 1 t 400 kg, 75 centners 50 kg, 6 t 4 centners; d. 1500 ml, 1 l 250 ml, 870 ml; e. lengths: 9 m 50 cm, 84 dm 7 cm, 18 m; f. weights: 80 centners 3 kg, 2500 kg, 3 centners 60 kg); round the measures approximately (a. 6 l 278 ml, 3 l 510 ml, 4 l 794 ml, 7 l 40 ml, 9 l 750 ml, 8 l 500 ml; b. 4 m 8 cm, 2 km 30 m, 8 dm 50 cm, 3 kg 900 g, 1 t 620 kg, 5 centners 50 kg, 6 g 700 mg); an activity: find a vessel's capacity: a. draw the table in your notebook (the table: the vessel's name / its capacity: the estimated measure, the measurement result, the difference: the example row: a food vessel — estimated 2 l, measurement result 3 l, difference 1 l); b. write the vessels' capacities you estimated in the table's 2nd column; c. measure the vessels' capacities using a 1-litre vessel or a divided vessel: write the measurement result in the table's 3rd column; d. find the difference between the estimated measure and the measurement result; most apartment households use water without measuring it: a household without a meter in a flat used 110 litres of water in a day (the text gives the metered household's day use for comparison): a. how many litres more water did the household without a meter use in one day than the household with a meter?; b. how many litres of water does one household use in 7 days?; c. what should we do in order to measure water?; a boxed task: at lunch the pupils of one class drank 10 litres of bilberry juice: each pupil drank 200 ml of bilberry juice: how many pupils does the class have (50)?; how much bilberry juice is needed for a class of 30 pupils (6 l)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 111 |
| Measuring length (Уртыг хэмжих): who gets to school by the shorter road (the map picture with the school and the two children's dashed routes: how many metres does each walk? When measuring with a ruler, count 1 cm as 200 m — a map scale); express the measure in the given unit (184 cm = □ m □ cm; 1500 m = □ km □ m; 1/5 km = □ m; 40 m 9 cm = □ cm; 5 km 10 m = □ m; 1/5 m = □ cm; a boy's unit box: 1 km = 1000 m; 1 m = 10 dm = 100 cm; 1 dm = 10 cm = 100 mm; 1 cm = 10 mm); find the given part of the measure (one-fifth of 1 kilometre; one-sixth of 60 metres; three-fourths of 1 metre; five-tenths of 100 metres); find the perimeters and express them in centimetres (the three pictured shapes with their sides in metres and half metres: the rectangle 4 m by 1/2 m; the stepped shape; the U-shaped fence); by how many different roads can the butterfly reach the flower: find each different road's length (the pictured curving paths measured 420 cm, 5 m 50 cm, 3 m 50 cm, 1/4 m and 4/10 m); the distance between two cities is 245 km: a cyclist travelled 1/5 of the whole road on the first day: on the second day, how many kilometres did he travel (the problem as posed asks for the second day from the journey's plan); two cities are 1245 km apart: a tourist travelled for three days: on the first day 2/5 of the whole road, on the second day 3/9 of the road remaining after the first day: on the third day, how many kilometres did he travel; from a rope 935 m long, two pieces of 235 m and 37 m were cut off: find the length of the rope remaining (663 m); a puzzle: a beetle climbs up 5 m by day and slides down 3 m by night on a tree 17 m tall: on which day does it reach the tree's top (the 7th day) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 91 |
| Measuring weight: centners and tonnes; balance puzzles — measuring heaviness (Хүндийг хэмжих): choose two by two from the following things and put them in the basket (a girl's unit box: 1 kg = 1000 g; 1 centner (ц) = 100 kg; 1 t = 1000 kg; 1 t = 10 centners; the pictured things with their weights: bread 1/2 kg, a toy car 4 kg 250 g, a book 1800 g, a carton of milk 1 kg, a box 350 g, an apple 1/5 kg, a toy figure 115 g, a notebook 1/10 kg: a. calculate the weight of each pair of things put in the basket; b. which pair of things in the basket has the greatest weight?; c. choose which two to put in so the basket's things have the greatest weight; d. can they be put in two by two so that the basket's things' weights are equal?); express the measure in the given unit (1/2 kg = □ g; 3/2 t = □ kg; 1/4 of a centner = □ kg; 500 kg = □ t; 15 centners = □ t); five packets of flower seed weigh 1/2 kg: how many grams is one packet of flower seed (100 g), and how many kilograms are 68 such packets (6.8 kg)?; a warehouse had 3400 kg of apples: 1/5 of them was distributed to a shop: find the measure of the apples remaining in the warehouse (2720 kg); the total weight of goods with their box was 1700 kg: from this, first 359 kg, then 408 kg and 154 kg were sold, and after that 719 kg of goods remained in the box: find the weight of the empty box (60 kg); a warehouse had 6370 kg of cabbage: from this, cabbage of equal measure was distributed to 7 shops, and after that 2/5 of the whole cabbage remained: how many kilograms of cabbage were distributed to each shop (546 kg); in one sack there is 3/10 of a centner of flour and in the other 7/10 of a centner: find the total measure of the sacks' flour (1 centner, 100 kg); weighing problems (Жинлтийн бодлого): 1. one of three identical-looking coins is lighter: how can it be picked out with a balance scale without weights (in one weighing)?; 2. one of nine identical-looking coins is lighter: how can it be found with a balance scale in 2 weighings?; assess your own skill (Өөрийн чадварыг үнэлсгээрэй): choose the tasks by your own skill level and test your skill: the easy level: 1. write the expressions and find their values (a. increase the quotient of 63832 and 202 by the product of 196 and 19; b. from 246184 subtract the product of 1246 and 108, and decrease the result by 1792); 2. the sum is 1386, and one number is 5 times greater than the other: find the two numbers (231 and 1155); 3. a pupil bought a notebook with 2/5 of his money and a pencil with 1/5 of it, and after that 100₮ was left to him: find the prices of the notebook and the pencil (his money was 250₮: the notebook 100₮, the pencil 50₮); 4. how many degrees is 3/4 of a straight angle, and what angle does it form (135°, an obtuse angle)?; 5. a square's perimeter is 20 cm: find its area measure (25 cm.kv); the middle level: 1. carry out the action (36 t 575 kg ÷ 77 + 19 t 108 kg − 6 t 913 kg — arithmetic with compound measures); 2. a herder has 240 head of livestock: 3/8 of all the livestock are horses, 7/10 of the rest are sheep, and the remainder are goats: how many goats are there (45)?; 3. what part of one day is 3 hours (1/8; and write 3 hours as a fraction of a day)?; 4. the sum of two numbers is 64: if one number is divided by the other, the quotient 3 and the remainder 4 come out: find these numbers (15 and 49); 5. compare (the compound measures: days against hours; tonnes and centners against centners; cm.kv against mm.kv; m.kv against cm.kv); the harder level: 1. the sum of three numbers is 1200: the I number is 2 times greater than the II number, and the III number is 400 more than the I number: find the numbers (160, 320 and 720); 2. one vessel holds milk worth 23500₮ and the other holds milk worth 16000₮: if one of the vessels held 15 litres more milk than the other, how many litres of milk were in each vessel (at 500₮ a litre: 47 litres and 32 litres)?; 3. with the digits 3, 8, 0, 6, how many three-digit numbers with different digits can be written (18)?; 4. 6 kg of paint was used to paint the floor of a room 6 m long and 4 m wide with oil paint: how much paint was used to paint 1 kv.m of floor (250 g)?; 5. express the area measures in square centimetres (5 m.kv; 700 mm.kv; 14 dm.kv; 16 m.kv) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 92–93 |
| Unit conversion tables: length, weight, area — observe the table and discuss (the conversion table of the measures: Length (Урт): 1 km = □ m = □ dm = □ cm = □ mm, with the chain factors 1000, 10, 10, 10 written under the links; Weight (Хүнд): 1 t = □ centners = □ kg = □ g = □ mg, with the factors 10, 100, 1000, 1000; Area (Талбай): 1 ha = □ ares = □ m.kv = □ dm.kv = □ cm.kv = □ mm.kv, with the factor 100 under each link; the rule box: "To express a big unit by a small unit, the number's value is multiplied by the suitable number. For example, 1 centner = 100 kg. To express a small unit by a big unit, the number's value is divided by the suitable number. For example, 100 cm = 1 m, 1000 m = 1 km"); find the equations' solutions ((□ + 12.4) − (15 − 9.8) = 7.2; 12.7 + □ = 15.8 + 13.1); a good's pure (net) weight is 31 kg and the box is the 2/10 part of the good's pure weight: find the weight of the boxed good (37.2 kg); find the measures (the table of rectangles with two of the length, width and perimeter given in mixed units — 8.5 m by 3.9 m; the width 4.8 dm with the perimeter 26.4 dm; the length 25.8 cm with the perimeter 94.6 cm; the length 184.12 dm with the perimeter 496.84 dm; 15.3 cm by 96.7 cm; 497 m by 372 m — the third measure to find each time); observe the dependence of a rectangle's length and width and draw a conclusion (a. a playground's rectangular field is 2.5 m long and 1.6 m wide: find the measure of the fence's length; b. if the playground's field is made into a square, how will the fence's length change? Compare them and find the difference); express the big unit by the small unit, using the table (7 dm = 70 cm, worked; 8 ha = □ ares; 4 t = □ centners; 9 m = □ cm; 3 dm.kv = □ cm.kv; 5 kg = □ mg); express the small unit by the big unit, using the table (2 cm = 2/100 m = 0.02 m, worked; 5 mm = □/□ cm = □ cm; 18 kg = □/□ centners = □ centners) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 114 |
| Measuring length (Уртыг хэмжих): an activity: measure the lengths of your finger (хуруу), сөөм, төө (span), тохой (cubit), дэлэм, алд (fathom) and алхам (pace): write the measures in your notebook and read them: are the measures the same as your teacher's and the other children's? Discuss (a. measure your desk's length with your төө and find it in centimetres, approximately; b. measure your class's length with your алд and your paces: count heel to heel, and decide whether the sole's measure should be added; c. a person's алд is approximately equal to their height: check this); at approximately what length and width are these things in real life (the pictured book, house, table and blackboard, with their boxes for the estimates)?; observe and explain the reason (the chain diagram of the length units, multiplying by 1000, 10, 10, 10 going down and dividing coming back; the relation box: 1 km = 1000 m; 1 m = 10 dm = 100 cm; 1 dm = 10 cm = 100 mm; 1 cm = 10 mm); write the measure that makes the inequality true (1 m < □ < 2 m; 8 cm < □ < 10 cm; 0.2 m < □ < 30 cm; 4 m < □ < 5 m); write the suitable divisions on the number line and express them in centimetres (the pictured 0 to 10 cm ruler with its arrows and boxes, 0.5 already marked); carry out the actions (1.5 m + □ cm = □ m; 1.2 km − 0.2 km = □ m; 700 cm ÷ 100 = □ mm; 5 dm × 2 = □ m; 1/5 dm + 4/5 dm = □ cm; 0.2 km + 0.8 km = □ m); Lake Baikal's depth is 1642 m: express this measure separately in dm, cm and km | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 129 |
| Measuring weight (Хүндийг хэмжих): for the things pictured, choose the suitable weight unit — gram, kilogram, centner or tonne — measure and write it (the school bag, the sack of flour, the sweets and the ring, each with its box); observe, think approximately, and explain the reason (the chain diagram of the weight units: 1 t to 1 centner ×10, 1 centner to 1 kg ×100, 1 kg to 1 g ×1000, and back by dividing; the relation box: 1 t = 10 centners = 1000 kg; 1 centner = 100 kg = 100000 g; 1 kg = 1000 g); express the measure in the unit stated (1.4 kg = □ g; 1.2 t = □ kg; 700 g = □ kg; 1/5 kg = □ g; 0.3 kg + 0.7 kg = □ kg; 0.2 t + 0.8 kg = □ kg); write the suitable number or measure (the two flow diagrams on which the arrows 200 kg × □, 0.7 t + □ and □ × 50 centners all lead to 1 t, with 0.5 t + □, □ × 500 kg and □ + 0.9 t leading out of it; and the arrows 2 centners ÷ □, □ × 4 kg and 200 kg ÷ □ leading to 1 centner, with 1 centner ÷ □, □ × 50 kg and 25 kg × □ leading out); observe the measure and complete the pattern (2 kg, 3000 g, 4 kg, 5000 g, 6 kg, □, 8 kg, □, □, and the traditional units 4 цэн, 5 лан, 6 цэн, □ лан, □ цэн, □ лан; the box of the traditional weight measures: 1 цэн = 3.73 g; 1 лан = 37.3 g; 1 унц (ounce) = 28.3 g; 1 жин = 596.8 g); find the weight of each fruit (the pictured balance scales: the green fruits balancing the 600 g weight; the fruits and the 500 g and 100 g weights; the oranges and the other fruits with the 1.7 kg and 1 kg weights — each fruit's weight to find); a logic puzzle: Bat, Bold, Dorj and Bilguun are 4 boys: Dorj is not the tallest, but Bat and Bold are taller; Bold is not taller than Bat: write the boys in order of height | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 130 |
| Measuring liquid volume: the litre and the millilitre — measuring the volume of a liquid (Шингэний эзлэхүүнийг хэмжих): by what unit is the measure of a liquid such as milk, tarag (yoghurt) or a drink measured (a boy's box: the Millilitre (Миллилитр): one millilitre's measure is approximately equal to 20 drops; a girl's box: the Litre (Литр): the millilitre is equal to the 1/1000 part of the litre; the relation bar: 1 litre = 1000 millilitres, and it is marked 1 l = 1000 ml); express the measure in the unit stated (67000 ml = □ l; 4500 ml = □ l; 3270 ml = □ l; 1954 ml = □ l — as decimal litres); express the measure in the unit stated (what part of one litre is 1 ml; what part of one litre is 50 ml; what part of one litre is 250 ml)?; from a vessel of 10 litres' capacity full of water, 1.5 l was taken, and a vessel of 450 ml capacity: how much water will remain in the vessel (8.05 l)?; in a glass vessel there was half a litre of water: after half a litre more was added to it, two fourths of a litre of the water was poured out of the vessel: what measure of water remained in the glass vessel (half a litre)?; to take 20 l of water from the tap, choose from the following vessels and write the possibilities: find the difference between the biggest-capacity and the smallest-capacity vessels (the pictured vessels of 9.5 l, 12 l, 8000 ml, 10 l 500 ml, 8 l and 3.2 l); because clean water's reserve is decreasing day by day through wasteful use, compose a problem from the information the boy read and solve it (the information boxes: if you collect water to wash the dishes, you save 90 litres of water in 10 minutes; if you shorten the time you spend in the shower by 3 minutes, you save 90 litres of water in a year; if you mend a broken tap, you save 518 litres of water in a day; if you collect water in a cup to brush your teeth, you save 6820 l of water in a year); if a tap is considered to spend 1385 ml of water unprofitably, make the calculation of the water spent unprofitably in one day, in 7 days and in a month; a puzzle: using only the 3-litre and 5-litre vessels, how can you measure out 4 litres of water at the river (the classic two-jug problem)? | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed p. 133 |
